## Sheet: `Sick Leave Paid By Employer`

_26 rows x 3 columns._

**Employer Sick Pay - Maintien de salaire par l'employeur (France, 2026)**

**Labour Code Article L1226-1 and Article D1226-1. France splits income replacement during sickness between the sickness insurance fund, which pays a daily benefit, and the employer, which tops it up. THE PAYSLIP THEREFORE CARRIES THREE LINES, NOT ONE: (1) the absence is deducted from the smoothed salary; (2) the daily social security benefit is added where the employer is SUBROGATED; (3) the employer top-up is added, computed on the pay the employee would have received LESS the daily benefit already counted.**

| Condition | Non-occupational sickness | Work accident / occupational disease |
|---|---|---|
| Service condition | ONE YEAR of service with the employer | ONE YEAR of service for the employer top-up; NONE for the daily benefit itself |
| Employer waiting period | SEVEN days | NONE - the top-up runs from the first day |
| Social security waiting period | THREE days before the daily benefit begins | NONE - the benefit runs from the first day |
| Employer top-up rate | 90% of the gross pay the employee would have received for the FIRST 30 DAYS, then 66.66% for the FOLLOWING 30 DAYS | Same 90% / 66.66% scale, with no waiting period |
| Extension for service | Each of the two periods increased by 10 DAYS PER FIVE YEARS of service beyond the first, up to a maximum of 90 days at each rate | Identical |
| Daily social security benefit | 50% of the daily base wage = average gross of the last three months / 91.25, the base CAPPED AT 1.4 TIMES THE MINIMUM WAGE (reduced from 1.8 by Decret n. 2025-160 of 20 February 2025) | 60% of the daily wage for the first 28 days and 80% FROM THE 29th, and the base is NOT subject to the 1.4 minimum-wage cap |
| Maximum gross daily benefit 2026 | EUR 41.95 from 1 February 2026 to 30 June 2026; EUR 42.97 FROM 1 JULY 2026 - the cap moved because the minimum wage rose on 1 June | No 1.4 minimum-wage cap applies |
| Paid leave accrual during the absence | 2 working days per month, CAPPED AT 24 per reference period (Act of 22 April 2024). The Court of Cassation held on 21 January 2026 that the cap is assessed reference period by reference period, without regard to leave carried over | The ORDINARY 2.5 working days per month, up to 30 |
| Collective agreement | Very frequently improves on the statutory scale - full pay from day one is common for managerial grades - and THE MORE FAVOURABLE RULE APPLIES | Identical |
| MAXIMUM DAILY BENEFIT FOR MATERNITY, PATERNITY, ADOPTION AND BEREAVEMENT LEAVE |  |  |
| EUR 104.02 for 2026. THESE BENEFITS USE A DIFFERENT AND MUCH HIGHER BASE than ordinary sickness, because they are calculated on the CAPPED MONTHLY WAGE rather than on the 1.4 minimum-wage cap. Reusing the sickness cap here understates the benefit by more than half. The supplementary birth leave introduced from 1 July 2026 follows the same subrogation logic. |  |  |
| RELATED RULES |  |  |
| Topic | Rule |  |
| Subrogation changes the payslip structure | Where the employer is SUBROGATED it maintains full pay, shows the daily benefit as an offsetting line and receives the benefit from the fund. Where it is NOT, the employee receives the benefit directly and the payslip shows only the deduction and the top-up. BOTH ARE LAWFUL, the collective agreement usually decides, and the choice must be recorded PER CLIENT AND PER ABSENCE TYPE. Declared in DSN block S21.G00.60 with the subrogation start and end dates and the employer's bank details. |  |
| Social and tax treatment of the two components | THE DAILY SOCIAL SECURITY BENEFIT is exempt from social security contributions but SUBJECT TO CSG AT THE REDUCED 6.20% RATE and to CRDS at 0.50%, and is FULLY TAXABLE - ordinary sickness benefits are NOT exempt, contrary to a common assumption. THE EMPLOYER TOP-UP is ORDINARY PAY IN EVERY RESPECT: all contributions, CSG and CRDS on 98.25%, and inside the net taxable amount. |  |
| Valuing the absence | The absence is valued with the REAL-HOURS method - monthly salary x hours not worked / hours the employee should have worked that month - which is the method preferred by the courts. Calendar days are used only for a hire or a departure, and the monthly social security ceiling must then be prorated by THE SAME METHOD. Using calendar days for a short absence under-deducts on a Monday and over-deducts on a Saturday and is the single most frequent source of unlawful deduction in French payroll audits. |  |
| Leave carried over after sickness | Leave that could not be taken because of sickness may be carried for FIFTEEN MONTHS, and that period runs ONLY from the day the employer informs the employee of the entitlement on return to work - THE EMPLOYER BEARING THE BURDEN OF PROVING IT GAVE THAT INFORMATION. Absent proof, THE LEAVE NEVER LAPSES and the compensatory leave indemnity on termination grows accordingly. |  |
| Beyond the employer top-up | Once the employer obligation is exhausted, income replacement passes to the sickness insurance fund and, on recognition of invalidity, to the invalidity pension. Any employer or scheme supplement paid afterwards is a separate wage type (62190) and is ordinary pay where the employer pays it; the exemption attaching to the statutory benefit does not extend to it. |  |
| Therapeutic part-time | Where a return to work on medical grounds is at reduced hours, the fund pays a partial daily benefit and the employer pays for the hours worked plus any agreement-based top-up. Declared in DSN block S21.G00.66 with its own start date, end date and amount (wage type 62878). |  |
| Wage types in this catalogue | 21108 salary reduction for sickness absence; 21190 salary reduction for long-term incapacity; 62873 employer sick pay top-up; 62875 daily sickness benefit under subrogation; 62876 work accident / occupational disease daily benefit; 62877 maternity, paternity and adoption daily benefit; 62878 therapeutic part-time top-up; 62968 guaranteed pay during a work accident; 62190 employer or scheme supplement during long-term incapacity. |  |

## Sheet: `Tax Credit`

_20 rows x 5 columns._

**Tax Credits and Reliefs (France, income year 2026)**

**FRANCE OPERATES NO TAX CREDIT AT PAYROLL LEVEL AT ALL. The employer applies a rate supplied by the tax administration to the net taxable amount and deducts the result: it performs no tax computation, applies no allowance, operates no progressive scale and grants no credit. This sheet therefore records the distinction that matters for configuration - PAYROLL-LEVEL reliefs, which change the net taxable amount or the contribution base and MUST be configured, against ASSESSMENT-LEVEL reliefs, which the tax administration applies to the annual return and which MUST NOT be built into a payroll engine. Building an assessment-level relief into payroll is a modelling error, and it is the error this sheet exists to prevent.**

| Relief | Level | Amount / rate (2026) | Eligibility | Application point |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Income tax exemption on overtime and additional hours | PAYROLL | EUR 7,500 NET per employee per calendar year, cumulative AND ACROSS EMPLOYERS. The amendments proposed to raise the cap to EUR 10,000 were not retained in the final 2026 Finance Act. | Every employee working overtime or, for a part-timer, additional hours; also days worked beyond an annual day-count arrangement. | Excluded from the net taxable amount BEFORE the withholding rate is applied. The year-to-date counter must be maintained and MIGRATED ON A PROVIDER CHANGE (wage type FR-027). |
| Employee old-age contribution relief on overtime | PAYROLL | The employee's EFFECTIVE statutory and compulsory supplementary old-age rate, CAPPED AT 11.31% (= 6.90% + 0.40% basic old-age, 3.15% Agirc-Arrco band 1, 0.86% general balance contribution band 1). Where the effective rate is lower, the lower rate applies. | The same hours as the income tax exemption. | Deducted from the employee's contributions. IT DOES NOT TOUCH CSG OR CRDS - overtime remains fully subject to the 9.70% (wage type FR-026). |
| Employer flat deduction on overtime hours | PAYROLL | EUR 1.50 per overtime hour below 20 employees; EUR 0.50 at 20 or more; SEVEN TIMES the hourly amount per additional day under a day-count arrangement. | ALL EMPLOYERS FROM 1 JANUARY 2026 - the previous 250-employee exclusion was removed by Article 21 of Act n. 2025-1403 of 30 December 2025. | Deducted from employer contributions, CAPPED so that it and the general reduction together cannot exceed the employer contributions actually due (wage type FR-025). |
| Unified degressive general reduction of employer contributions | PAYROLL | C = Tmin + Tdelta x [0.5 x (3 x 21,876.40 / annual gross - 1)] ^ 1.75, with Tmin = 0.0200, Tdelta = 0.3781 (FNAL at 0.10%) or 0.3821 (FNAL at 0.50%), coefficient rounded to four decimals. The annual reference of EUR 21,876.40 is FROZEN at the 1 January SMIC of EUR 12.02 for the whole of 2026. | Annual gross pay BELOW EUR 65,629.20, increased by any value-sharing bonus paid in the year. | Imputed against the employer contributions it covers, INCLUDING 0.49 points of the AT-MP contribution and the Agirc-Arrco contributions. CLIFF OF TWO POINTS at the threshold, not a taper (wage type FR-024). |
| Sector-specific flat deduction (DFS) | PAYROLL - contribution base only | A percentage reduction of the CONTRIBUTION BASE, capped at EUR 7,600 per employee per year, falling by one point a year toward abolition - 24% for journalists in 2026. | The occupations listed in Article 5 of the annex to the Decret of 17 November 1936, where the agreement provides for it AND the employee has consented, renewed annually. | Gross pay is FIRST increased by reimbursed professional expenses, THEN reduced by the percentage. IT DOES NOT REDUCE THE INCOME TAX BASE (wage type FR-045). |
| Apprentice and intern income tax exemption | PAYROLL | The ANNUAL MINIMUM WAGE per calendar year (General Tax Code Article 81 bis). | Apprentices and interns. | Excluded from the net taxable amount until the cumulative threshold is reached; beyond it the pay enters the net taxable amount in full. |
| Intern gratuity exemption from contributions | PAYROLL | 15% of the HOURLY social security ceiling per hour of attendance = EUR 4.50 PER HOUR FOR 2026, the hourly ceiling being EUR 30. | Interns under a tripartite internship agreement. An intern is NOT an employee and is outside the minimum wage entirely. | Only the EXCESS above the threshold is a contribution base (wage type 62900). |
| Inbound-assignee regime (impatries) | PAYROLL for the premium | The premium is exempt for its REAL AMOUNT or, at election, at a FLAT 30% of total net remuneration. The combined exemption of the premium and the foreign-work portion is capped, at election, at 50% of total remuneration or at 20% of taxable remuneration excluding the foreign-work portion. | Recruited abroad by a French company or transferred within a group; NOT tax-resident in France in the FIVE calendar years before taking up the post; becomes tax-resident in France. | Excluded from the net taxable amount MONTH BY MONTH, to 31 December of the EIGHTH calendar year following the year of taking up the post. INCOME TAX ONLY - the premium stays in every social base (wage types 62320, 62330, 62334). |
| Expatriation supplement (Article 81 A) | PAYROLL | Exempt within 40% of the remuneration the employee would have received for the same work in France, where the supplement is fixed in advance and separately from the salary. | Employees posted abroad by a French employer, meeting the duration condition. | Excluded from the net taxable amount. SOCIAL SECURITY IS DECIDED SEPARATELY, on the coordination instrument and not on the tax exemption (wage type 62310). |
| Flat 10% professional-expense deduction | ASSESSMENT - DO NOT BUILD | Minimum EUR 509, maximum EUR 14,555 for 2026 (2025 income). | Every employee automatically, unless actual expenses are claimed instead. | APPLIED BY THE TAX ADMINISTRATION TO THE ANNUAL RETURN, NEVER BY THE EMPLOYER. It influences the rate the employer later receives and nothing else. NOTE: a 10% flat deduction DOES apply at payroll level in ONE case only - the Article 182 A non-resident withholding base. |
| Actual professional expenses (frais reels) | ASSESSMENT - DO NOT BUILD | No overall cap; each category has its own rules, with the published mileage scale for vehicle use. Commuting beyond 40 km each way needs justification. | Any employee whose evidenced expenses exceed the flat deduction. | Itemised with the annual return; evidence kept for three years. The employer plays no part. |
| Journalists' allowance for employment expenses | ASSESSMENT - DO NOT BUILD | EUR 7,650, withdrawn where annual gross income exceeds EUR 93,510. | Professional journalists holding a press card. | Replaces the 10% flat deduction in the annual return. NOT a payroll relief, and NOT the same thing as the sector-specific flat deduction of the contribution base. |
| Minimum assessment rate for non-residents (Article 197 A) | ASSESSMENT - DO NOT BUILD | 20% up to EUR 29,579 of French net taxable income and 30% above; 14.4% and 20% for overseas-source income. Displaceable by proof of a lower worldwide rate. | Non-residents. | RECORDED HERE AS A NEGATIVE. It operates at ASSESSMENT level and MUST NOT be modelled in the payroll engine, in the wage type catalogue or in the data dictionary. The employer's obligation for a non-resident is the Article 182 A withholding and nothing else (wage type FR-074). |
| NO EMPLOYER YEAR-END INCOME TAX RECONCILIATION IS PERFORMED IN FRANCE |  |  |  |  |
| There is no employer year-end reconciliation of income tax, no annual return of employee income tax to file, and no year-end certificate the employer must compute. France does NOT annualise: the employer applies a rate to the amount actually paid in the period and makes no projection, no estimate of full-year income and no cumulative recalculation. Implementation teams migrating from a cumulative jurisdiction routinely build projection logic that France neither needs nor permits. What the employer does maintain cumulatively is the year-to-date net taxable amount and the year-to-date tax withheld, both reported in the DSN and shown on the payslip - neither feeds back into the current period's rate. Only TWO cumulative counters genuinely affect the calculation: exempt overtime against the EUR 7,500 cap, and exempt apprentice or intern pay against the annual minimum wage. FOUR YEAR-END ADJUSTMENTS ARE COMPULSORY ON THE SOCIAL SIDE, and all four must be run in December OR IN THE FINAL PAYSLIP for a leaver: the progressive ceiling reconciliation; the annual reconciliation of the unified degressive reduction; the capping of the 1.75% CSG allowance at four annual ceilings, with recovery of any excess already granted; and the reconciliation of exempt overtime against the EUR 7,500 cap. |  |  |  |  |

## Sheet: `Global WTC Coverage`

_392 rows x 5 columns._

**Global WTC V1.0 Coverage Confirmation - France**

**ALL 300 Pay Element Codes of shared/Global_WTC_V1_0.xlsx are present in the Wage Type Catalogue sheet, IN BASELINE ORDER, at catalogue rows 2 to 301 (row = baseline position + 1). None was skipped, none was duplicated and none was excluded. Rows 302 to 375 are the 74 France-specific additions, which carry no global code. Every global code was configured FRESH FOR FRANCE from FR-CCG-001-v1.0: no treatment was copied from another country, and the same global code carries a materially different configuration here from the one it carries in the delivered Belgium catalogue.**

## Sheet: `Form Reference Guide`

_51 rows x 5 columns._

**Form Reference Guide - wage type category to statutory report row (v2.4 C17)**

**Built by cross-referencing the wage type categories of the Wage Type Catalogue sheet against the fifteen delivered specs in FR-compliance/04-reports-spec/. ROW NUMBERS ARE THE ACTUAL 1-INDEXED ROWS of the named sheet in the named workbook, read from those files and not inferred. Where a category reaches a report only through an aggregate rather than through a row of its own, the Notes column says so rather than asserting a row that does not carry it.**

| Wage Type Category | Spec File | Sheet Name | Row Number | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Base salary (62100-62109, 62205) | FR-DSN-001-DSNMensuelle-Spec.xlsx | DSN Mensuelle Spec | 288 | Remuneration type S21.G00.51.011 = "010 - Salaire de base", amount at row 290. Type 010 EXCLUDES every salary supplement - seniority bonus, holiday bonus, thirteenth month, benefits in cash and in kind, occasional overtime. Required in every monthly DSN by control CCH-11. |
| Base salary (62100-62109) | FR-DSN-002-FinContratUnique-Spec.xlsx | FCTU Spec | 146 | The end-of-contract signal requires at least three remuneration blocks: types 001, 002 and 010 (control CCH-15). Amount at row 148. |

Hours at row 289, amount at row 290. |

Amount at row 408. |

| Seniority (62140-62142, 62180-62182) | FR-DSN-001-DSNMensuelle-Spec.xlsx | DSN Mensuelle Spec | 441 | Seniority block S21.G00.86: type at 441, unit of measure at 442, value at 443. Seniority drives the seniority bonus, the notice period, the redundancy formula and the employer sick-pay extension, and is declared rather than derived by the recipient. |
| Configuration flags (FR-057 to FR-062, FR-067) | FR-DSN-001-DSNMensuelle-Spec.xlsx | DSN Mensuelle Spec | 288 | The IDCC, the managerial grade, the PCS-ESE code, the contract nature and the place-of-work identifier are declared in the CONTRACT block (S21.G00.40) and the place-of-work block (S21.G00.85, INSEE municipality code at row 439), NOT in an amount-bearing row. They drive calculation but carry no amount, so no amount row is asserted for them. |
